Applicable Codes, Standards and Regulations 
The project will be assessed based on the local authority requirements and the applicable building safety codes and standards as listed below. Dubai Civil Defence (DCD) is the Authority Having 
Jurisdiction (AHJ) for this project. The primary life safety code is the UAE Fire and Life Safety Code of Practice (UAEFC-2018) and it is our understanding from previous experience that DCD permits 
the use of international standards such as NFPA 5000 and NFPA 101 as supplementary design guidelines. Where there are inconsistencies between UAEFC and NFPA, the local requirements will be 
applicable for this project.

CONSTRUCTION AND COMPARTMENTATION 
 
 
Fire Rating Requirements with respect to Property lines or other Buildings 
Based on NFPA 5000, and Section 2.7 of the 2018 UAE Fire and Life Safety Code, buildings will be required to maintain a minimum horizontal distance from each other. If the minimum distance is not 
met, any exterior wall within this distance will be required to be fire-rated and unprotected openings in that building face will be required to be protected or limited in size. Where two or more 
buildings are located on the same plot, the horizontal separation will be measured from the building face to an imaginary line drawn between the exterior walls of the adjacent buildings. The relevant 
parts of Table 1.3 of 2018 UAE Fire and Life Safety Code are reproduced in the following table:  
 
 
Figure 2: Measurement of Horizontal Distance

Table 5: Fire Resistance Separation 
It is noted that in case a compartment fire rating and structural fire rating applies to the same fire wall or compartment, the higher rating should be applicable. For examples, for any stair enclosure 
as part of the core support, the higher fire rating for the structure should be applied as per type of construction assessment. However, the stair enclosure opening is only required to be maintained 
for 90 minutes. 
Fire Protection Rating of Windows and Doors 
Every opening in a fire separation/ fire barrier should be protected to limit the spread of fire and to restrict the movement of smoke from one side of the fire barrier to the other. Fire doors in all 
fire separations should be provided with fire-protection ratings in accordance with UAE Fire Code, Chapter 1, Table 1.10. Refer to Table 7 of this report for more details. 
Fire windows If provided, the combined area of glazing in fire rated window assemblies and fire rated door assemblies used in fire barriers should not exceed 25 percent of the area of the fire barrier 
that is common with any room. 
All rated doors should be provided with self-closing devices and latching. Panic hardware if provided should comply with UAE Fire Code.

Penetrations into, and openings through, an exit enclosure assembly should be limited to the following:

Fire Dampers 
In accordance with the UAE Fire Code, Fire and/or smoke dampers should be provided where ductwork penetrates or terminates at openings in walls or partitions required to achieve a fire or smoke 
resistance performance. All fire and/or smoke dampers should have a fire rating that is equivalent to the performance rating of the wall or partition in which it is to be installed. 
Where a fire barrier is also constructed as a smoke partition, a combination fire/smoke damper designed and tested in accordance with the requirements of ANSI/UL 555, Standard for Fire Dampers, 
and ANSI/UL 555S should be installed. 
Motorised fire / smoke rated dampers should be provided within the air supply system to separate the return air portion in the event of a fire.  
The minimum fire protection rating, as specified in Table 1.13 of the UAE Fire Code and reproduced in table below. 
 
Fire Damper Rating 
Fire Resistance Rating of Assembly 
Minimum Damper Rating 
3-hours or greater fire resistance−rated assemblies 
3-Hours 
Less than 3-hours fire resistance−rated assemblies 
90-minutes 
Table 6: Fire Damper Rating 
In accordance with Chapter 1, Section 2.15.1.3, UAE Fire Code, fire dampers should be required in the following locations: 
 
• Ducts and air-transfer openings, penetrating walls or partitions having a fire resistance rating of 2 or more hours 
• Ducts and air-transfer openings, penetrating shaft walls having a fire resistance rating of 1 or more hours 
• Ducts and air-transfer openings, penetrating floors that are required to have protected openings where the duct also is not protected by a shaft enclosure 
• Air-transfer openings that occur in walls or partitions that are required to have a fire-resistive rating of 30-minutes or more.  
Smoke Dampers 
In accordance with Table 10.1, Chapter 10 of UAE Fire Code, the following are the areas where Smoke Dampers are required. 
 
• HVAC duct branching serving apartments, dwelling units. 
• Smoke dampers should be installed at or adjacent to the point where air ducts pass through required smoke barrier, but in no case smoke damper shall be installed more than 0.6m from the barrier, 
or after the first air duct inlet or outlet, whichever is close to the barrier. 
• If the air handling equipment is serving more than one floor then smoke damper shall be installed in system with a capacity greater than 7080L/s (15,000ft3/min) to isolate the air handling 
equipment, including filters from the remainder of the system on both the building supply and return side, in order to restrict the circulation of smoke. 
• Smoke damper used for protection of openings in smoke barriers or in engineered smoke control systems shall be classified in accordance with ANSI/UL 555S, and with minimum class II leakage 
rating, and elevated temperature rating shall not be less than 2500 C

Building Services in Fire Separations 
Penetrations for cables, cable trays, conduits, pipes, tubes, combustion vents and exhaust vents, wires, and similar items to accommodate electrical, mechanical, plumbing, and communications 
systems that pass through a wall, floor, or floor-ceiling assembly constructed as a fire separation, will be required to be protected by a fire-stop system or device. The fire-stop system or device will 
be tested in accordance with ASTM E 814, "Standard Test Method for Fire Tests of Through-Penetration Fire Stops", or UL 1479, "Standard for Safety for Fire Tests of Through-Penetration Fire Stops".  
Fire-stop systems will not be required to be provided where concrete, grout, or mortar has been used to fill the annular spaces around cast-iron, copper, or steel piping, or steel conduit or tubing, 
that penetrates one or more concrete or masonry fire resistance–rated assemblies. 
 
The nominal diameter of each penetrating item will not be permitted to exceed 150 mm, and the opening size will not be permitted to exceed 92,909 mm2 (1 ft2). The thickness of the concrete, 
grout, or mortar will be required to be the full thickness of the assembly. Examples of fire stop are indicated in the Figures below:

[REGION 4/6]
### VERBATIM TEXT

Building Services in Fire Separations

Penetrations for cables, cable trays, conduits, pipes, tubes, combustible systems that pass through a wall, floor, or floor-ceiling assembly construction shall be tested in accordance with ASTM E 814, "Standard Test Method for Firestop Systems."

Fire-stop systems will not be required to be provided where concrete or masonry that penetrates one or more concrete or masonry fire resistance-rated assemblies.

The nominal diameter of each penetrating item will not be permitted to exceed 100 mm. Grout, or mortar will be required to be the full thickness of the assembly.

### DRAWING DESCRIPTION

This image contains a section of text from a document discussing building services in fire separations. The text outlines the testing requirements for penetrations through fire-rated assemblies and specifies that fire-stop systems are not required where concrete or masonry penetrates fire resistance-rated assemblies. It also mentions that the nominal diameter of each penetrating item should not exceed 100 mm and that grout or mortar must be used to fill the full thickness of the assembly. The text is part of a larger document, as indicated by the reference to "Figure 3" in the top right corner.

Interior Finishes 
In accordance with NFPA 101 Section 10.2.3.4 and NFPA 255, Products required to be tested in accordance with ASTM E84, Standard Test Method for Surface Burning Characteristics of Building 
Materials, or ANSI/ UL 723, Standard for Test for Surface Burning Characteristics of Building Materials shall be classified as follows in accordance with their flame spread and smoke development: 
 
1. Class A interior wall and ceiling finish are characterized by the following: 
• Flame spread, 0–25 
• Smoke development, 0–450 
No continued propagation of fire in any element thereof when so tested 
 
2. Class B interior wall and ceiling finish are characterized by the following:  
• Flame spread, 26–75 
• Smoke development, 0–450 
 
3. Class C interior wall and ceiling finish are characterized by the following:  
• Flame spread, 76–200 
• Smoke development, 0–450 
 
Interior floor finishes shall be classified based on test results from NFPA 253, Standard Method of Tests for Critical Radiant Flux of Floor Covering Systems Using a Radiant Heat Source or ASTM E 648 
Standard Test Method for Critical Radiant Flux of Floor Covering Systems Using a Radiant Heat Energy Source.  
 
(1) Class I interior floor finish shall be characterized by a critical radiant flux not less than 0.45 W/cm2. 
(2) Class II interior floor finish shall be characterized by a critical radiant flux not less than 0.22 W/cm2 but less than 0.45 W/cm2. 
 
Based on the requirements of UAE Fire Code Table 1.12 and with the provision of an automatic sprinkler system throughout the proposed project, the interior wall, ceiling and floor finish shall be as 
follows:

It should be noted that DCD inspectors at the time of inspection are requesting one hour fire resistance rating test certificate for any wooden panel installed in exit corridor or public corridors/ 
lobbies. The same requirement applies to FHC wooden doors, if any are installed. A 90-minute fire-rated door will be required. 
Mezzanine  
In accordance with Chapter 1, Table 1.9, UAE Fire Code 2028, following shall be noted:   
 
i. 
Mezzanines construction shall be with 1 hour fire resistance rating. The aggregate area of mezzanines within a room, other than those located in special-purpose industrial occupancies, shall not 
exceed one-third the open area of the room in which the mezzanines are located. 
ii. 
Enclosed space shall not be included in a determination of the size of the room in which the mezzanine is located. 
iii. 
There shall be no limit on the number of mezzanines in a room. 
iv. 
the area of the mezzanines shall not be included in the area of the room. 
v. 
All portions of a mezzanine, other than walls not more than 1065 mm high, columns, and posts, shall be open to an unobstructed exit from the room in which the mezzanine is located, unless 
the occupant load of the aggregate area of the enclosed space does not exceed 10. 
vi. 
A mezzanine having two or more means of egress shall not be required to open into the room in which it is located if not less than one of the means of egress provides direct access from the 
enclosed area to an exit at the mezzanine level.

### VERBATIM TEXT

It should be noted that DCD inspectors at the time of inspection are lobbies. The same requirement applies to FHC wooden doors, if any are.

**Mezzanine**

In accordance with Chapter 1, Table 1.9, UAE Fire Code 2028, follow:

i. Mezzanines construction shall be with 1 hour fire resistance rating.
ii. The area of the mezzanines shall not exceed one-third the open area of the room in which the mezzanine is located.
iii. Enclosed space shall not be included in a determination of the size of the mezzanine.
iv. There shall be no limit on the number of mezzanines in a room.
v. the area of the mezzanines shall not be included in the area of the room.
vi. All portions of a mezzanine, other than walls not more than 100 mm thick, shall be included in the occupant load of the aggregate area of the enclosed space of the mezzanine.
vii. A mezzanine having two or more means of egress shall not be required to provide a means of egress from the enclosed area to an exit at the mezzanine level.

MEANS OF EGRESS 
 
In accordance with UAE Fire Code, Chapter 3 Section 1.1, a means of egress is a continuous and unobstructed way of travel from any point in a building or structure to a public way consisting of three 
separate and distinct parts. Means of egress is expanded to comprise of three separate and distinct parts: 
• Exit access is a portion of means of egress that leads to an exit. 
• Exit is a portion of means of egress that is separate from all other spaces of a building to provide a protected way of travel to the exit discharge. 
• Exit discharge is a part of means of egress between termination of an exit and public way. 
Number of Means of Egress 
Accordance with Chapter 3, Table 3.14 of UAE Fire Code, each floor level or room is required to be provided with sufficient number of exit width/exit capacity to accommodate the occupant load. 
The minimum required numbers of exits are listed in the Table below.

#### MEANS OF EGRESS

In accordance with UAE Fire Code, Chapter 3 Section 1.1, a means of egress is comprised of separate and distinct parts. Means of egress is expanded to comprise:
- Exit access is a portion of means of egress that leads to an exit.
- Exit is a portion of means of egress that is separate from all other portions.
- Exit discharge is a part of means of egress between termination of exit access and exit.

For the proposed high-rise sprinklered project, the minimum exit separation of one-third (1/3) of the floor diagonal will be complied with at all levels. Further details are provided in FLS drawings. 
Exit Discharge 
In accordance with Chapter 3, Table 3.12, UAE Fire Code, exit discharge is providing occupants with a safe path of travel from exit to public way and should comply with the requirements below: 
• For sprinkler protected buildings, minimum of 50% of the required number of exits, and minimum of 50 % of the required egress capacity, should discharge directly to the outside of the building 
through yards, courts, open spaces, or similar spaces to open to sky, leading to public way or directly to a public way. 
• In sprinklered buildings, not more than 50% of the required number of exits, and not more than 50% of required exit capacities are permitted to discharge through interior building areas such as 
lobbies and corridors. 
• The interior exit discharge should lead to a free and unobstructed way to the exterior of the building, and such way should be readily visible and identifiable from the point of discharge from the 
exit. 
• Direct discharge should be directly towards the public way and not at the back of the building where occupants still need to travel across the building exterior to reach point of safety of public 
way. 
Impediments to Exit Discharge 
Arrangement of the means of egress should be in accordance with the following requirements: 
• Access to an exit should not pass-through kitchens, store rooms, restrooms, closets, bedrooms or similar spaces or other rooms or spaces subject to locking. 
• Exit access and exit doors should be designed and arranged to be clearly recognizable. 
• Corridors should provide exit access without passing through any intervening rooms other than corridors, lobbies and other spaces permitted to be open to corridor. 
Stair Landings 
In accordance with Chapter 3, Table 3.4, UAE Fire Code, stairs and intermediate landings should continue with no decrease in width along the direction of egress travel. In buildings, every landing 
should have a dimension, measured in the direction of travel that is not less than the width of the stair. The maximum permitted height of each flight of stairs between landings is 3660mm. Stair 
landings should be solid, without perforations. Stairs should be constructed of RCC (reinforced concrete). 
The clearance between the leading edge of the opening door leaf and stair newel post should be required not less than half of the minimum required width of the exit stair refer to the graphics that 
follows.

### VERBATIM TEXT

For the proposed high-rise sprinklered project, the minimum exit separation shall be in accordance with the following:

#### Exit Discharge

In accordance with Chapter 3, Table 3.12, UAE Fire Code, exit discharge shall be as follows:
- For sprinkler protected buildings, minimum of 50% of the required number of exits shall discharge through yards, courts, open spaces, or similar spaces to open to sky.
- In sprinklered buildings, not more than 50% of the required number of exits shall discharge into lobbies and corridors.
- The interior exit discharge should lead to a free and unobstructed exit.
- Direct discharge should be directly towards the public way and not through any other way.

#### Impediments to Exit Discharge

Arrangement of the means of egress should be in accordance with the following:
- Access to an exit should not pass through kitchens, store rooms, rest rooms, or similar spaces.
- Exit access and exit doors should be designed and arranged to be operable from the inside.
- Corridors should provide exit access without passing through any impediments.

#### Stair Landings

In accordance with Chapter 3, Table 3.4, UAE Fire Code, stairs and fire escapes shall have a dimension, measured in the direction of travel that is not less than 1.2 meters.

| Exit Discharge | Exit discharge requirements for sprinkler protected buildings. |
| Impediments to Exit Discharge | Restrictions on the arrangement of means of egress. |
| Stair Landings | Minimum dimension for stairs and fire escapes. |

Stair Riser and Tread Depth 
In accordance with Chapter 3, Table 3.2, UAE Fire Code, the rise of steps should be required to be between 100mm and 180mm. The minimum tread depth should be required to be 280mm. The riser 
height should be required to be measured between two subsequent leading edges of steps. Stair tread depth should be solid, without perforations. 
In accordance with Chapter 7, Section 7.2.2.3.6, NFPA 101, variation in excess of 4.8mm in the depth of adjacent treads or in the height of adjacent risers should be prohibited. The tolerance between 
the largest and smallest riser or between the largest and smallest tread should not exceed 9.5mm in any flight. 
 
 
 
Figure 9: Measurement of Riser and Tread depth 
Stair Headroom Clearance 
In accordance with Chapter 3, Table 3.4, UAE Fire Code, the minimum clear height inside staircases, above steps, should be required to be 2030mm measured vertically above a plane parallel to, and 
tangent with, the most forward projection of the stair tread with a tolerance of 19mm, above the finished floor. In other components of means of egress such as corridors and exit passageways the 
headroom should not be less than 2285mm, with projections from the ceiling not less than 2030mm. nominal above the finished floor of steps. 
The minimum ceiling height should be maintained for not less than two-thirds of the ceiling area of any room or space, provided that the ceiling height of the remaining ceiling area is not less than 
2030 mm. 
 
 
Figure 10: Measurement of Headroom Clearance

Handrails 
In accordance with Chapter 3, Table 3.4, UAE Fire Code, Stairs and Ramps should have handrails on both sides.  Handrails should be provided within 760mm of all portions of the required egress width. 
Handrails on stairs and ramps should not be permitted to be less than 865mm and not more than 965mm above the surface of the tread, measured vertically from a line drawn through the outside 
edges of the nosing or from the surface of a ramp. The maximum projections of handrails allowed in the required width is 100 mm on each side. 
The height of required handrails that form part of a guard should be permitted to exceed 965mm, but should not exceed 1065mm, measured vertically to the top of the rail from the leading edge of 
the tread. 
Handrails should be installed to provide a clearance of not less than 57mm between the handrail and the wall to which it is fastened. 
Handrails should be required to have a circular cross section with an outside diameter of not less than 32mm and not more than 51mm or a shape that is other than circular with a perimeter dimension 
of not less than 100mm, but not more than 160mm, and with the largest cross-sectional dimension not more than 57mm, provided that graspable edges are rounded so as to provide a radius of not 
less than 3.2mm. 
Handrails that are not continuous between flights should be extended horizontally 305 mm outside the top riser and continue to slope for a depth of one tread outside the bottom riser. Handrails ends 
should be returned to the wall or floor or should terminate at newel posts.  
 
  
 
 
 
 
Figure 11: Handrail and Stair Width Measurement 
 
 
 
 
 
Figure 12: Wall Mounted Handrail Measurement 
     
Guards 
In accordance with UAE Fire Code, Chapter 3 Table 3.1, guard requirements are as follows: 
• Guards should be provided for every elevated means of egress, open landing for stairs where elevated surface is more than 760mm from finished ground level. 
• Such guards should not encroach the required clear width of the stair. 
• The height of guards should be measured vertically to the top of the guard from the surface adjacent thereto. 
• Guards should be not less than 1200 mm high. In case of stair or ramp handrails that form part of a guard, the height of the guard can be reduced to 1065 mm. 
• Open guards should have intermediate rails or an ornamental pattern up to a height of 865 mm, such that a sphere 100mm in diameter is not able to pass through any opening. 
• The triangular openings formed by the riser, tread, and bottom element of a guardrail at the open side of a stair should be of such size that a sphere 150mm in diameter is not able to pass through 
the triangular opening.

[REGION 1/6]
### VERBATIM TEXT

In accordance with Chapter 3, Table 3.4, UAE Fire Code, Stairs and Ramps, Handrails on stairs and ramps should not be permitted to be less than 100mm from the edges of the nosing or from the surface of a ramp. The maximum projection of the handrail should be 100mm. The height of required handrails that form part of a guard should be not less than 965mm from the top of the tread.

Handrails should be installed to provide a clearance of not less than 50mm from the wall. Handrails should be required to have a circular cross section with an outer diameter of not less than 100mm, but not more than 160mm, and with the largest dimension of the handrail not more than 3.2mm.

Handrails that are not continuous between flights should be extended to the wall or floor or should be returned to the wall or floor or should terminate at newel posts.

### DRAWING DESCRIPTION

The drawing depicts a section of a staircase with handrails. The handrails are shown extending from the wall and are circular in cross-section. The handrails are labeled with dimensions and specifications. The wall is shown on the left side of the drawing. The handrails are labeled with the following dimensions:

- Maximum projection: 100mm
- Handrail diameter: 32-51mm
- Minimum stair clear width: 1200mm
- Maximum height: 1065mm
- Minimum handrail height: 965mm
- Maximum diameter of handrail: 160mm
- Largest dimension of handrail: 3.2mm

The drawing also includes a note indicating that the handrail should not project more than 100mm from the wall. The handrails are shown extending from the wall and are labeled with the dimensions and specifications mentioned above.

### VERBATIM TEXT

Stairs and Ramps should have handrails on both sides. Handrails should be provided with a height to be less than 865mm and not more than 965mm above the surface of the tread, measured vertically. The maximum projections of handrails allowed in the required width is 100 mm on each side. The handrail should be permitted to exceed 965mm, but should not exceed 1065mm, measured vertically. There should not be less than 57mm between the handrail and the wall to which it is fastened. The handrail should be in conformation with an outside diameter of not less than 32mm and not more than 51mm or a shape with the largest cross-sectional dimension not more than 57mm, provided that grasping is possible. The handrail should be extended horizontally 305 mm outside the top riser and continue to slope for a depth of 100mm to terminate at newel posts.

Corridors 
In accordance with Chapter 3, Table 3.24 UAE Fire Code, the residential floors; corridors shall have a minimum 1-hour fire resistance rating. In addition, grilles and louvers shall be prohibited in walls 
or doors of exit access corridors. 
The egress capacity of the corridor shall not be less than the required capacity of the exit to which the corridor leads. In accordance with Chapter 3, Table 3.24 UAE Fire Code, corridor width is 
tabulated below: 
 
Corridor Width 
Occupancy 
Required Width 
Residential, Assembly 
1200 mm 
 
Table 11:Corridor Width 
Ramps 
In Accordance with UAE Fire Code, Table 3.9: 
• All ramps serving as required means of egress shall be of permanent fixed non-combustible construction.  
• The ramp floor and landings shall be solid and without perforations. 
• Ramps shall have landings located at the top, at the bottom, and at doors opening onto the ramp.  
• Every landing shall have a width not less than the width of the ramp.  
• Where the ramp is not part of an accessible route, the ramp landings shall not be required to exceed 1220 mm in the direction of travel, provided that the ramp has a straight run.  
• Any changes in travel direction shall be made only at landings.

Change in Level 
In accordance with Table 3.1 of UAE Fire Code, requirements are as follows: 
• Abrupt changes in elevation of walking surfaces should not exceed 6.3 mm. 
• Changes in elevation above 6.3 mm to 13 mm, should be beveled with a slope of 1 in 2. 
• Changes in elevation exceeding 13 mm but not in excess of 535 mm shall be considered a change in level and should be achieved by either a ramp with maximum slope of 1 in 12 or stair with tread 
depth not less than 330 mm. 
• Changes in level in means of egress should be achieved by an approved means of egress where the elevation difference exceeds 535 mm. 
 
BALCONY TERRACE AND WINDOW 
Minimum Construction Requirements for Balconies and Railings 
In accordance with UAE Fire Code, Chapter 1, Section 2.17.2, Guards are required for any space, walking or standing which is elevated 760 mm above the finished grade level.  
From the finished floor level, height of the balcony, terrace railings, elevated space railings should not be less than 1200 mm.  
For balusters openings, the separation distance between vertical posts or members of balcony, guardrail or handrail shall not be more than 100 mm.  
Any opening formed by either vertical posts, curved frames or design features in the balcony or railings shall not be more than 100 mm.  
Horizontal elements, bars, climbable features shall not be installed up to 760 mm. 
If the design demands any gap between finished floor surface and the bottom most horizontal component of the railing, such gap shall not be more than 100 mm.  
The balcony, Handrail, Guard assembly should be able to withstand a single concentrated load of 200 pounds (0.89 KN), applied in any direction at any point.  
If the design demands usage of glass panels in the balcony construction, such glass shall be reinforced glass which can withstand a load of 200 pounds (0.89kN), applied in any direction at any point.  
Balconies and terraces should not have accesses with self-closing or self-latching doors, which can accidentally lock people outside in the balcony or terrace, compelling them to misadventures such 
as climbing, descending or crossing over to other balconies.

In accordance with Table 3.1 of UAE Fire Code, requirements are as follows:
- Abrupt changes in elevation of walking surfaces should not exceed 6.3 mm.
- Changes in elevation above 6.3 mm to 13 mm, should be beveled with a depth not less than 330 mm.
- Changes in elevation exceeding 13 mm but not in excess of 535 mm, should be achieved by an appropriate ramp with a depth not less than 330 mm.

Minimum Construction Requirements for Windows 
In accordance with UAE Fire Code, Chapter 1, Section 2.17.3, Openable Windows shall open outward from the top.  Openable windows shall not open from below. 
Sliding and openable Windows shall not be located at (F), a height less than 865 mm from the finished floor surface level.  If window base is present on the finished floor, the height (F) shall be 
measured from the top of such window base. See Figure 15a. The window pane, if openable, shall not create a gap of more than 100 mm from the wall, when opened from the top.  
The window assembly, shall have reinforced or safety glass and shall be able to withstand a single concentrated load of 200 pounds (0.89 kN), applied in any direction at any point. 
There should not be any construction features at the bottom of the window which can be exploited into ‘ladder or climbing feature’. Provide locks and safety measures for sliding windows.  See 
Figure 15b.

DOOR  
Doors 
In accordance with Chapter 3, Table 3.2, UAE Fire Code, every door and door assembly shall be required to be designed and constructed so that the way of egress travel is obvious and direct.  
In accordance with Chapter 3, Table 3.2, the UAE Fire Code, doors opening in means of egress will be required to be not less than 915 mm in clear width. Where a pair of doors is provided, not less 
than one of the doors should have 810 mm clear width. Exit access door assemblies serving a room not exceeding 6.5m2 and not required to be accessible to persons with severe mobility impairments 
shall be not less than 610mm in door leaf width. 
 
The door assembly shall be readily operable from the egress side without special knowledge or effort. The forces required to fully open any door leaf manually in a means of egress shall not exceed:                   
• 67 N (15 lbf) to release the latch,                                                                                                
• 133 N (30 lbf) to set any door leaf in motion,                                                                    
• 67 N (15 lbf) to open any door leaf to the minimum required width 
 
For swinging door assemblies, clear width will be required to be measured as follows: 
• The measurement should be taken at the narrowest point in the door opening.  
• The measurement should be taken between the face of the door leaf and the stop of the frame. For swinging door assemblies, the measurement should be taken with the door leaf open 90 degrees. 
• Projections of not more than 100mm into the door opening width on the hinge side should not be considered reductions in clear width, provided that such projections are for purposes of 
accommodating panic hardware or fire exit hardware and are located not less than 865mm and not more than 1220mm above the floor.  
• Projections exceeding 2030mm above the floor should not be considered reductions in clear width. 
 
The elevation of the floor surfaces on both sides of a door opening shall not vary by more than 13mm and it should be maintained so for not less than width of widest door leaf.  Thresholds at door 
openings should not exceed 13mm in height. Where doors serve spaces that are not normally occupied, the floor level will be permitted to be lower than that of the door opening but should be not 
more than 205mm lower. 
Balcony and terrace access shall be restricted with door having child lock or latch. Window opening should be restricted to 100mm opening with windows safety latch. Every closet door latch should 
be such that children can open the door from inside the closet. Every bathroom door should be designed to allow opening from the outside during an emergency when locked. At all times, all locking 
devices that impede or prohibit egress or that cannot be easily disengaged shall be prohibited.

### VERBATIM TEXT

DOOR

Doors

In accordance with Chapter 3, Table 3.2, UAE Fire Code, every door assembly shall be readily operable from the egress side with the following forces:
- 67 N (15 lbf) to release the latch,
- 133 N (30 lbf) to set any door leaf in motion,
- 67 N (15 lbf) to open any door leaf to the minimum required width.

For swinging door assemblies, clear width will be required to be measured at the narrowest point in the door opening:
- The measurement should be taken at the narrowest point in the door opening,
- The measurement should be taken between the face of the door leaf and the door frame,
- Projections of not more than 100mm into the door opening width are allowed to accommodate panic hardware or fire exit hardware and are located not more than 1000mm above the floor,
- Projections exceeding 2030mm above the floor should not be considered.

The elevation of the floor surfaces on both sides of a door opening shall not exceed 13mm in height. Where doors serve spaces more than 205mm lower than the floor on which they open, the floor surface on the lower side shall be at least 13mm below the floor surface on the higher side.

The text does not include any drawings or diagrams.

### VERBATIM TEXT

Every door and door assembly shall be required to be designed and constructed so that, in the event of fire, doors opening in means of egress will be required to be not less than 915 mm in width. Exit access door assemblies serving a room not exceeding 6.5m2 and not required to be fire rated shall be not less than 760 mm in width.

The forces required to fully open any door shall not exceed 100 N.

The required width

shall be measured as follows:

At the point in the door opening.

At the edge of the door leaf and the stop of the frame. For swinging door assemblies, the measurement of the opening width on the hinge side should not be considered reductions in clear width. The clear widths and are located not less than 865mm and not more than 1220mm above the floor.

The clear width shall not be considered reductions in clear width.

The clear width for opening shall not vary by more than 13mm and it should be maintained so for not less than 1000mm. If doors serve spaces that are not normally occupied, the floor level will be permitted to be lowered to a maximum of 100mm below the finished floor level of the space served.

Door Swing 
In accordance with Chapter 3, Table 3.2 UAE Fire Code, all doors in a means of egress shall be of the side hinged or pivoted swinging type and should be installed to be capable of swinging from any 
position to the full required width of the opening in which it is installed. Doors will be required to swing in the direction of egress travel were serving a room or area with an occupant load of 50 or 
more or serving horizontal exit or serving high hazard content room or serving an exit enclosure. 
Doors serving Residential Units shall not be required to swing in the direction of egress. 
During its swing, any door in a means of egress shall leave not less than one-half of the required width of an aisle, a corridor, a passageway, or a landing unobstructed and should project not more 
than 180 mm into the required width of an aisle, a corridor, a passageway, or a landing, when fully open. Refer to figure below:

Self-Closing and Automatic Closing Devices 
In accordance with Chapter 3, Table 3.2, UAE Fire Code, doors normally required to be kept closed shall not be secured in the open position at any time and shall be self-closing or automatic closing. 
Doors should be permitted to be provided with automatic-closing/hold-open devices, where the following conditions apply: 
1. Upon release of the hold-open mechanism, the door becomes self-closing. 
2. The release device is designed so that the door instantly releases manually and upon release, becomes self-closing. 
3. The automatic releasing mechanism or medium is activated by the operation of approved smoke detectors. 
4. Upon loss of power to the hold-open device, the hold-open mechanism is released, and the door becomes self-closing. 
5. The release by means of smoke detection of one door in a stair enclosure results in closing all doors serving that stair. 
In accordance with Table 3.3a UAE Fire Code, self-closing door is required on the following locations: 
• Garbage room 
• Elevator Lobby 
• Exit stairs 
• All fire rated doors

TRAVEL DISTANCE 
Maximum Travel distances are measured from the most remote point of the floor to a nearest fire protected fire exit. Travel distance requirements are directly in relation to the different occupancy 
classification and use. There are three (3) travel distance criteria as part of the means of egress design, as follows; 
• Common Path of Travel 
• Dead-end corridor 
• Travel Distance 
The following are descriptions of the above components: 
Common Path of Travel 
As a general principle, a common path of travel exists where a space is arranged so that occupants within that space are able to travel in only one direction to reach any of the exits or to reach the 
point at which the occupants have the choice of two paths of travel to remote exits, see figure below: 
This common path limit applies to areas within all spaces, up to a point where an occupant can go in two directions.  
Common path does not apply inside residential units. 
 
 
 
Figure 19: Common path of travel

FIRE FIGHTING ACCESS 
General Requirements for High-rise Building 
• As the project is categorized as Highrise buildings, therefore in accordance with Table 2.5 Chapter 2 of UAE Fire code, the extend of fire engine access-way shall be as illustrated in table below:  
 
Extent of Fire Vehicle Access for High-rise Building (with Sprinklers) 
High-rise Buildings 
Extent of Fire Access  
Less than 4000 m2   
Minimum of 1/6 perimeter (at least 15 meters) 
4001 m2 to 8000 m2 
1/4 perimeter 
8001 m2  to 16,000 m2 
1/2 perimeter 
16,000 m2  to 32,000 m2 
3/4 perimeter 
More than 32,000 m2  
Whole perimeter (100%) 
 
Table 17: Extent of the fire vehicle for High-rise Building 
The proposed project provides fire truck access along 100% of the building perimeter. The access road is located not less than 2 meters and not more than 10 meters from the building façade (except 
for one area on the right side which will be coordinated with the Architectural Team at the next stage). The access road is 6 meters wide and shall be maintained free of any obstructions, including 
trees, fixed furniture, landscaping elements, or other permanent structures. Furthermore, no canopies, balconies, or other overhead projections shall encroach upon the required emergency access 
route. 
Fire Truck Access (FTA) is provided around the entire plot; however, in order to reach the building perimeter within the required 2–10 meter distance from the façade, the fire truck may need to 
travel against the direction of the road (in reverse). This arrangement will be subject to approval by Dubai Civil Defence (DCD). 
 
Note: The plot will be surrounded by large trees, palm trees, and tall shrubs of different heights planted at a minimum separation distance of 10 meters, which may create potential obstructions for 
firefighters during emergency operations. This condition will be subject to review and confirmation by the inspection team. 
Fire Access for Fire Vehicle 
• The minimum width of the firefighting access-way shall be 6 m throughout and must be able to accommodate the entry and maneuvering of a fire engine, extended ladders, pumping appliances, 
aerial appliances, turntable and/or hydraulic platforms.  
• The fire engine access-way shall be provided within 18 m of the breeching inlet with a maximum parking distance of Civil Defence vehicle from building entrance shall not exceed 15 m. 
• The Fire engine access-way shall be located at least 2 m from the building, but not more than 10m away from the external wall or façade of the building. 
• The fire engine access-way shall be laid on a level platform or if on an incline, the gradient shall not exceed 10%. 
• Public roads can serve as fire engine access provided that the locations of such public roads are in compliance with the fire engine access requirements. 
• The access-way and fire engine access roads shall be kept clear of all obstructions and other parts of the building, plants, trees or other fixtures shall not obstruct the path between the access-
way and the access openings. 
• A suitable access pathway shall also be provided to enable fire-fighters to inspect all elevations of a building during or after a fire. A suitable pathway could be a paved or gravel path. Any such 
pathway shall be a minimum of 1 m wide and Plants, Bushes, Walls or other features shall not impede such pathway. 
• Fire access roadways and fire access way shall be free of obstruction and designated parking for fire vehicles shall be available at all times. Signs shall be provided with ‘NO PARKING, FIRE LANE’ 
and ‘PARKING RESERVED OF CIVIL DEFENCE’ signage. Size of wordings shall not be less than 50 mm.

### VERBATIM TEXT

The proposed project provides fire truck access along 100% of the building perimeter. However, for one area on the right side which will be coordinated with the Architect, the fire truck access will be provided through trees, fixed furniture, landscaping elements, or other permanent structures. The fire truck access will be provided through a designated route.

Fire Truck Access (FTA) is provided around the entire plot; however, the fire truck will be required to travel against the direction of the road (in reverse). This arrangement will be subject to approval by the Fire Authority.

Note: The plot will be surrounded by large trees, palm trees, and tall shrubs. This condition will be subject to approval by the Fire Authority.

Emergency Command Centre (ECC) 
For the high-rise buildings emergency command center shall be provided with adequate size to house all the terminals and supervisory/control equipment, etc. of the building’s fire 
protection/detection systems and should have a free working space of at least 19m2 with no dimension less than 3000mm. ECC shall be located at the entrance side of the building and along fire 
access road in accordance with Chapter 2 Section 2.9.3 of UAE Fire Code. 
The ECC must be very close to the fire-fighting lift lobby or at the main entrance lobby.  Air conditioning or mechanical ventilation required for the ECC shall be provided with a secondary power 
supply and should have ductworks independent of any other ductwork serving other parts of the building.    
The ECC shall contain the following: 
 
1. Building plan indicating floor plans and detail locations of building exit staircases, means of egress, exit routes, protected corridors and exit access, fire protection systems, fire compartments, 
fire-fighting equipment and fire department access. 
2. Emergency Voice/Alarm communication system unit. 
3. Fire Detection and alarm system unit. 
4. Annunciator visually indicating the location of the elevators and their operation. 
5. Status indicator and control of air handling system. 
6. Controls for unlocking electromagnetics or access control stairway doors. 
7. Sprinkler valve and water flow detector display panels. 
8. Emergency and standby power status indicators. 
9. Fire Pump status indicators. 
10. Generator supervision devices and manual start and transfer features. 
11. Public address system. 
12. Controls for smoke management systems.  
 
Fire Fighting Lifts 
In accordance with Chapter 2, Section 2.9.4 UAE Fire Code, since the accommodation buildings is classified as a high-rise, it shall be provided with a firefighting lift. The fire-fighting lift shall be 
required to be provided with emergency power and fire-fighting controls as per ASME A17.1. The fire-fighting lift shall be required to comply with Dubai Municipality requirements. Fireman’s lift car 
shall have a minimum size and arrangement to accommodate ambulance stretcher of 610mm X 2134mm with not less than 127 mm radius corners, in the horizontal, open position. 
The fire lift shall have access to every habitable floor above or below the designated floor.  The fire lift shaft should be provided with an operational feature that would enable firemen to cancel first 
or earlier call which has been inadvertently made to the fire lift during an emergency. 
All elevators should return to the recall level after fire alarm activation. If a fire is detected at the recall level, the elevator should be recalled to an alternate recall level. 
 
FIRE FIGHTING SYSTEMS 
 
This section of the report identifies the fire-fighting systems required in the proposed building. The required fire suppression systems shall be provided in accordance with NFPA 101, UAE Fire Code 
and other applicable NFPA standards and codes.  
Sprinkler System 
In accordance with Table 9.18 of UAE Fire Code, the high-rise building shall be protected throughout the building by an approved automatic sprinkler system. As per UAE Fire Code and NFPA 13 
requirements, the sprinkler system shall comply with the following:  
The maximum area of any single sprinkler zone should be limited to 4,831 m2 for Light and Ordinary Hazard areas. Where the floor area exceeds this limit, additional water supply risers should be

The minimum operating pressure of any sprinkler for determining the water supply requirements should not be less than 0.5 bar. The maximum operating pressure in a sprinkler system should not 
exceed 12 bar. Electrical rooms may not be sprinklered if the all the following conditions apply:  
 
• The room is dedicated to electrical equipment only. 
• Only dry-type electrical equipment is used. 
• Equipment is installed in a 2-hour fire-rated enclosure including protection for penetrations. 
• No combustible storage is permitted to be stored in the room

Foam Sprinkler System 
A deluge foam-water sprinkler system shall be provided for the generator room. A deluge system is a fixed fire protection system in which the piping system is dry until the deluge valve operates to 
distribute pressurized foam-water from the open nozzles. 
The system piping should be hydraulically designed to obtain reasonably uniform foam and water distribution and to allow for loss of head in system piping. A minimum of 3% foam-water solution 
should be discharged. 
 
Figure 25: Typical Foam Water Sprinkler System for Diesel Generator Room 
 
Standpipe (Wet Riser) System 
In accordance with Chapter 9, Table 9.1 UAE Fire Code, a wet riser system should be required for the entire building. 
The wet riser system should be provided with Class III system. A Class III system fire hose station should consist of a 25 mm hose reel or 40 mm fire hose rack & a 65mm diameter landing valve with 
hoses and nozzles. The hose stations should be located next to each stair enclosures at typical floors and beside each exit way at the ground floor. 
Additionally, a fire hose station along the exit access corridor or horizontal exit or in the car park floors should be installed such that all portions of each floor are within 30 m from the fire hose 
station while measured along the natural path of travel from the hose station. 
The wet risers serving this building should be required to be designed in accordance with NFPA 14, “Standard for the Installation of Standpipe and Hose Systems”. The Fire Department connections 
(breaching inlet) for the sprinkler system and wet riser should be required to be provided along the sides of the building along the fire truck access road. 
When most remote landing valve is located at a height 45 m from the fire pump location, the risers should be zoned into “High Zone” and “Low Zone” with looping.  
 
Fire Pumps 
Based on UAE Fire Code requirements, the building shall be provided with a dedicated fire pump set to serve the water-based fire protection system. The typical fire pump set consists of a jockey 
pump, main electric motor-driven fire pump and standby Diesel-engine fire pump.  The fire pumps shall be designed, installed and tested for acceptance in accordance with NFPA 20 requirements.  
Fire Water Tank 
The fire water supply shall be from a permanent water storage tank to cater to the pumping demand. The water tank should be separated into two equal half compartments (50% + 50%) such that

even during water tank cleaning process in one compartment, another compartment is available for the fire protection systems. The fire water tank should be sized to cater the fire-fighting systems 
for a duration of 60-minutes, complete with automatic low water level detection and instantaneous refilling arrangement. 
 
Figure 26: Horizontal Fire Pump for Illustration 
Breeching inlets 
It is required that the breeching inlets are located on the street side and visible from the fire engine access. A clear path without obstruction to the inlets is required and it is a good practice to 
provide a walkway with 1200 mm in width from the fire engine access road. Breeching inlets shall be located at a visible main entrance side of the building. The breeching inlet will be required to be 
within 18m of the fire truck accessway. Breeching inlets shall consist of i. Four 65 mm, (4-Way) direct coupling type fitting for wet riser. ii. Two 65 mm, (2-Way) direct coupling type fitting for 
Sprinkler Riser. iii. Two 65 mm, (2-Way) direct coupling type fitting for Dry riser. It shall be located not less than 457 mm nor more than 1219 mm above the finished ground level. 
Portable Extinguishers 
In accordance with Chapter 4, Section 2.1, UAE Fire Code, as a first line of defence during the initial stages of a fire the availability of portable fire extinguishers is mandatory for all occupancies to 
be used by the occupants to extinguish the fire before the fire grows out of control.

SMOKE MANAGEMENT SYSTEMS 
Stair Pressurization 
In accordance with Chapter 10 Table 10.4 of UAE Fire Code, for the proposed building as habitable height exceeding 23 m, all exit stairs enclosures serving the building shall be pressurized and meet 
the requirements for a smoke proof enclosure.  
The key criteria for the stair pressurization system are as follows: 
• Shall be an approved engineered system. 
• Shall achieve the design pressure difference across the barrier with no less than 12.5 Pa for sprinklered building. 
• Shall be no more than force of 133 N for the pressure difference across the door. 
• The air velocity at the supply air inlets shall not exceed 5 m/s and the air velocity through the open door on the floor of fire shall not be less than 1 m/s. 
• The calculation shall consider 2 No. of doors to be opened simultaneously (one door at the floor of fire origin and the discharge door at the grade level). 
• Standby fans will not be required. 
Lift Pressurization 
Lift hoist way pressurization shall be provided for all passenger and fire lifts in high-rise buildings, in accordance with Section 2.19, Chapter 10 of the UAE Fire and Life Safety Code of Practice, 2018. 
Corridor Smoke Purging 
Corridor and open circulation areas shall be provided with mechanical smoke purge with a mechanical system. The intent of the smoke purge system is for the smoke removal for firefighting operations 
during and after fire. 
The key criteria for corridor smoke purging system are as follows: 
• Smoke extract fans should be approved for effective operation at 400°C for 2 hours. 
• Corridor smoke purge mechanical system shall be capable of achieving a minimum of 6 air changes per hour. 
• Mechanical air supply inlets should achieve a minimum 85% airflow rate of the extract. 
• The supply points for the makeup air should be located beneath the smoke layer interface. 
• Air supply inlets and smoke extraction outlets should be distributed alternately, such that the horizontal distance between the supply and extraction, measured along the axis of circulation, should 
not exceed 15 m. 
• Extraction outlet shall not be located within 6 meters of any exit stair or exit. 
• When a smoke extraction outlet is served by two air supply inlets, the distance between inlets and out let’s must be equivalent.  
• Smoke extraction outlets should have their lower part at least 1.830 m above the floor. 
• Back-up (Stand-by) fans should not be required for corridor and open circulation area smoke management systems. 
• Corridor and open circulation area smoke management system, control panel, automatic smoke curtains, if any, Fans and related equipment should be provided with an emergency power.

[REGION 1/6]
### VERBATIM TEXT

SMOKE MANAGEMENT SYSTEMS

Stair Pressurization

In accordance with Chapter 10 Table 10.4 of UAE Fire Code, for the purpose of the requirements for a smoke proof enclosure.

The key criteria for the stair pressurization system are as follows:
- Shall be an approved engineered system.
- Shall achieve the design pressure difference across the barrier with a maximum of 50 Pa.
- Shall be no more than force of 133 N for the pressure difference across the barrier.
- The air velocity at the supply air inlets shall not exceed 5 m/s and shall be no more than 1000 N.
- The calculation shall consider 2 No. of doors to be opened simultaneously.
- Standby fans will not be required.

Lift Pressurization

Lift hoist way pressurization shall be provided for all passenger and freight lifts.

Corridor Smoke Purging

Corridor and open circulation areas shall be provided with mechanical smoke extraction during and after fire.

The key criteria for corridor smoke purging system are as follows:
- Smoke extract fans should be approved for effective operation at the maximum design pressure difference across the barrier.

### DRAWING DESCRIPTION

This image contains text related to smoke management systems, specifically focusing on stair pressurization, lift pressurization, and corridor smoke purging. The text outlines the requirements and criteria for these systems as per the UAE Fire Code. There are no drawings or diagrams present in the image.

[REGION 2/6]
### VERBATIM TEXT

For the proposed building as habitable height exceeding 23 m, all exit stairs enclosed as follows:

1. Fire barrier with no less than 12.5 Pa for sprinklered building.
2. Pressure difference across the door.
3. Air velocity not exceed 5 m/s and the air velocity through the open door on the floor of fire shall not be less than 5 m/s.
4. Doors shall be opened simultaneously (one door at the floor of fire origin and the discharge door at the floor of fire origin).

[REGION 4/6]
### VERBATIM TEXT

Corridor and open circulation areas shall be provided with mechanical smoke purging system during and after fire.

The key criteria for corridor smoke purging system are as follows:

- Smoke extract fans should be approved for effective operation at [illegible] during and after fire.
- Corridor smoke purge mechanical system shall be capable of achieving a minimum airflow of 85%.
- Mechanical air supply inlets should achieve a minimum 85% airflow.
- The supply points for the makeup air should be located beneath the smoke extraction outlets.
- Air supply inlets and smoke extraction outlets should be distributed such that the distance between them does not exceed 15 m.
- Extraction outlet shall not be located within 6 meters of any exit sign.
- When a smoke extraction outlet is served by two air supply inlets, the distance between them should not exceed 15 m.
- Smoke extraction outlets should have their lower part at least 1.8 m above the floor.
- Back-up (Stand-by) fans should not be required for corridor and open circulation areas.
- Corridor and open circulation area smoke management system, complies with the above criteria.

Miscellaneous Rooms Smoke Control 
The following rooms require a dedicated mechanical ventilation system. Rooms should be temperature controlled dedicated AHU as recommended by the equipment and control panel manufacturers. 
• Fire Pump Room 
• Generator Room 
• Emergency/Fire Command Centre 
 
Mechanical ventilation system should comply with the following requirements: 
• The mechanical ventilation system should be an independent system of exhaust mode only exclusive to the particular area. 
• Exhaust should not be into underground, basement or car park spaces. 
• Exhaust should be directly to the exterior, through dedicated ducts or shafts and should not be less than 5 m from any air intake openings. 
• Where the exhaust ducts run outside the area they are serving, they should either be enclosed in a structure or be constructed to give at least the same fire rating as the area they are serving or 
that of the room through which they traverse, whichever is higher. The rating should apply to fire exposure from both internal and external of the duct or structure. 
• Ducts serving systems and areas other than the mechanical ventilated areas should not pass through such rooms. 
• Standby power is required for the mechanical ventilation systems serving as smoke exhaust. 
 
Figure 29: Ducts Serving other Areas than Mechanically Ventilated Areas

• Exit routes, Exit stairs, Exit corridors, Exit lobbies 
• Public or assembly areas                                                                                                          
• Public seating areas                                                                                                                             
• Firefighting lobbies, Fire Command Center, Fire pump room, Control Rooms                                            
• Lift shafts, Firefighting riser shafts, Sprinkler valve rooms, garbage chutes, cavity walls, HVAC ducts

### VERBATIM TEXT

Miscellaneous Rooms Smoke Control

The following rooms require a dedicated mechanical ventilation system:
- Fire Pump Room
- Generator Room
- Emergency/Fire Command Centre

Mechanical ventilation system should comply with the following requirements:
- The mechanical ventilation system should be an independent system.
- Exhaust should not be into underground, basement or car park spaces.
- Exhaust should be directly to the exterior, through dedicated ducts.
- Where the exhaust ducts run outside the area they are serving, they should be at least as high as the roof of the room through which they traverse, whichever is higher.
- Ducts serving systems and areas other than the mechanical ventilation system should be isolated.
- Standby power is required for the mechanical ventilation systems.

As per UAE Fire Code Chapter 11 Table 11.4, LPG Piping Distribution, Filling and Other Requirements are as follows:  
• 6 m distance shall be maintained from tank surface to any Air intake units, Window type and Split Type AC units, Air vents, balanced type flue outlets.  
• 10 m distance shall be maintained from tanks to Fire Hydrant, firefighting equipment, firefighting valves, fire command centers. 
• LPG fill line and connection arrangement shall be fully exposed outside the building. Where need arises to cover it aesthetically, fully ventilated, or fully perforated enclosure is acceptable.  
• Filling connections shall be electrically classified areas without any ignition sources within 7.6 m. 
    
FIRE DETECTION & ALARM SYSTEM 
 
In accordance with NFPA 101 Section 30.3.4.1.1, Apartment buildings four or more stories in height or with more than 11 dwelling units shall be provided with a fire alarm system.   
In accordance with Chapter 8 Table 8.13 UAE Fire and Life Safety Code, as proposed project is classified high-rise building for accommodation buildings, smoke detection and alarm system shall be 
provided throughout the building. 
In accordance with NFPA 101 Section 30.3.4.1.1 and 9.6.1.7, a complete fire alarm system shall provide functions for initiation, notification, and control, which shall perform as follows: 
• Initiation - The initiation function provides the input signal to the system. 
• Notification - The notification function is the means by which the system advises that human action is required in response to a particular condition. 
• Detection & Control - The control function provides outputs to control building equipment to enhance protection of life. 
Initiating Devices for Fire Alarm System 
The initiation of the fire alarm system shall be by any or all of the following means: 
• Activation of automatic smoke and / or heat detectors 
• Activation of a manual call point 
• Operation of an extinguishing system 
• As a result of the fire alarm system not being reset during an investigation period 
Automatic Detectors 
Smoke, heat and multi-sensor detectors shall be provided throughout the apartment building, service rooms, auxiliary rooms & common areas and located in accordance with NFPA 72. As a minimum 
these shall be installed inside apartments, corridors, lobbies, gymnasiums and assembly areas.  
 
Pump rooms, gymnasiums involving dusts or vapours shall be protected by heat-detection devices. Refer Figure below shows the placement of smoke detectors and heat detectors in smooth ceilings.

10 m distance shall be maintained from tanks to Fire Hydrant, fire fighting equipment, etc.
LPG fill line and connection arrangement shall be fully exposed outside the building.

In accordance with NFPA 101 Section 30.3.4.1.1 and 9.6.1.7, a complete fire alarm system shall be provided.
Initiation - The initiation function provides the input signal to the fire alarm system.
Notification - The notification function is the means by which the fire alarm system communicates the alarm condition.
Detection & Control - The control function provides outputs to control the fire alarm system.

Initiating Devices for Fire Alarm System

The initiation of the fire alarm system shall be by any or all of the following:
Activation of automatic smoke and / or heat detectors
Activation of manual pull stations
Activation of fire detection devices
Activation of fire extinguishing systems
Activation of sprinkler systems
Activation of other fire protection systems

Smoke Alarms 
In accordance with NFPA 101, Section 30.3.4.5 smoke alarms shall be installed in every sleeping area, outside every sleeping area in the immediate vicinity of the bedrooms, and on all levels of the 
dwelling unit. 
Manual Call Points 
The manual call points shall be installed on all escape routes and beside stairwell entrances and all exits to open air for all buildings in the project. The manual call points shall be installed so that 
they are conspicuous, unobstructed, and accessible. Distribution of the manual call points should be such that travel distance should not be more than 61 m to reach the nearest manual call point.  
Two-Way Communication System 
As per UAE Fire Code 2018, following should apply:  
• Two-way communication system shall be installed for communication between the emergency/fire command centre (ECC) and: 
o 
Inside stair, on every floor landing 
o 
Inside each elevator cars 
o 
Inside Fire Pump Room 
o 
Inside Fire Lift Lobby 
o 
Inside Area of Refuge, if any 
• At least two handsets shall be stored at each control center for use by emergency responders. 
• Each telephone station or telephone jack shall be clearly and permanently labeled to allow the caller to identify his or her location to the control center by voice. 
 
As per NFPA 72 Sec 7.14.3.4, the following should apply to the two-way communication service: 
• The system should be listed as a two-way telephone communication service.

Fire Alarm Control Panel 
As per UAE Fire Code, Chapter 8, Table 8.13 - Main FACP shall be located at the continuously attended location such as emergency/fire command centre or reception or Security guard house, as per 
the building fire strategy. 
 
All components of the fire alarm system shall be coordinated to ensure that the main fire alarm panel can receive a fire and/ or fault signal from all areas as well as from the fire suppression systems 
prior to initiating an evacuation signal and activating fire related plant. 
As per UAE Fire Code, Chapter 8 Table 8.13 For High-rise Building - Additional sub FACP’s or annunciators complete with all functional features shall be provided at the elevator lobby of the floor or 
the mechanical floor at every 45m height of the building and connected to main FACP in a network. 
 
The repeater and annunciator panels, if provided shall be connected to the main Fire Alarm Control panel.

Fire Alarm Control Panel

As per UAE Fire Code, Chapter 8, Table 8.13 - Main FACP shall be located in accordance with the building fire strategy.

All components of the fire alarm system shall be coordinated to ensure that the fire alarm system is fully operational prior to initiating an evacuation signal and activating fire related plant.

As per UAE Fire Code, Chapter 8 Table 8.13 For High-rise Building - A FACP shall be located on the mechanical floor at every 45m height of the building and connected to the Main FACP.

The repeater and annunciator panels, if provided shall be connected to the Main FACP.

EMERGENCY LIGHTING AND EXIT SIGNAGE 
Emergency Lighting 
The required emergency lighting and exit signage shall be provided in accordance with the latest UAE Fire Code Chapter 6.  
Emergency Lighting System shall be provided for the building. Emergency Lighting is required for egress paths and emergency response and service areas including 
 
• Inside Fire Pump Room, EPS Room, and Large Plant Rooms  
• Inside and Outside Emergency Command Center  
• Inside and Outside Stairways  
• Inside Lift Lobby  
• Inside Fire Fighting Lift Lobby  
• In Exit Corridors  
• Inside Exit Access Corridors  
• Inside Exit Passageways  
• All Areas of enclosed Basements and Ramps  
• In Service Corridors  
• Outside Exit Discharge  
• Reception  
• Location of FACP  
• Location of Fire Hose Cabinet  
• Location of Fire Extinguishers  
• Location of Smoke Control Panel  
• LPG Tank Location 
• Inside LV Room  
• Inside Transformer Room  
 
Emergency lighting shall provide sufficient illumination to: 
• Indicate clearly and unambiguously the way out along pre-determined escape routes 
• Indicate and illuminate fire alarm call points and fire extinguisher locations along escape routes 
 
The emergency lighting system shall be arranged to provide the required illumination automatically in the event of any interruption of normal lighting for a minimum of 3 hours because of any of the 
following occurring: 
• Failure of a public utility or other outside power supply. 
• Opening of a circuit breaker or fuse. 
• Manual act[s], including accidental opening of the central normal lighting facilities. 
 
Initial illumination shall be not less than an average of 10.8 lux with a minimum of 1.1 lux at any point measured along the path of egress at floor level and shall not fall to an average of 0.65 lux after 
1.5 hours. The maximum to minimum illumination uniformity ratio shall be designed not to exceed 40 to 1. The delay period between failure of normal lighting and energising the emergency lighting 
system shall not exceed 10 second.

[PAGE 43]
Page | 43 
Exit Signage 
In accordance with Chapter 5, of UAE Fire Code, exits signs on the following location: 
1. The entrance to every exit on every floor shall be clearly indicated by an exit sign placed over the exit door. Such signs shall be placed so that they are always clearly visible. 
2. In long corridors, in open floor areas and in all situations where the location of the exits may not be readily visible, directional signs shall be provided to serve as guides from all portions of the 
corridors or floors. 
3. Exits, other than main exterior exit doors that obviously and clearly are identifiable as exits, exits shall be marked by an approved sign that is readily visible from any direction of exit access. 
4. Horizontal components of the egress path within an exit enclosure shall be marked by approved exit or directional exit signs where the continuation of the egress path is not obvious. 
5. Access to exits shall be marked by approved, readily visible signs in all cases where the exit or way to reach the exit is not readily apparent to the occupants. A sign with a directional indicator 
showing the direction of travel shall be placed in every location where the direction of travel to reach the nearest exit is not apparent to the occupants. 
6. Exit Signage shall be always illuminated. 
 
STANDBY EMERGENCY POWER 
 
The standby power system shall be a Type 60, Class 2, Level 1 system compliant with NFPA 110. Generators and battery systems shall be located in a room separated from the remainder of the building 
by fire barriers having a minimum 2-hour fire resistance rating. Wiring for power and control shall be located and properly protected to ensure a minimum 1 hour of operation in the event of a fire.   
In accordance with DCD requirements the following standby power loads must be connected to the emergency generator and must be operational within 60 seconds. 
 
• Fire service elevators 
• Emergency mechanical air handling systems (i.e., smoke proof enclosures and smoke management systems) 
• Electric fire pumps (where both main & standby pumps are electrical motor driven) 
• Emergency command centre equipment and lighting 
The following systems shall operate with no more than 10 seconds of transfer time between normal power and emergency (standby) power, batteries, or UPS: 
• Exit signs and means of egress illumination. 
• Elevator car lighting 
• Fire detection and alarm system 
• Sprinkler alarm system 
• Smoke control system 
• Voice communication system 
 
Any UPS system installed in the building shall be compliant with NFPA 111.

[REGION 1/6]
### VERBATIM TEXT

#### Exit Signage

In accordance with Chapter 5, of UAE Fire Code, exits signs on the following:
1. The entrance to every exit on every floor shall be clearly indicated.
2. In long corridors, in open floor areas and in all situations where the direction of travel is not obvious, exit signs shall be provided at the entrance to corridors or floors.
3. Exits, other than main exterior exit doors that obviously and clearly indicate the direction of travel, shall be marked by approved, readily visible signs.
4. Horizontal components of the egress path within an exit enclosure shall be marked by approved, readily visible signs.
5. Access to exits shall be marked by approved, readily visible signs showing the direction of travel shall be placed in every location where access to the exit is not obvious.
6. Exit Signage shall be always illuminated.

#### STANDBY EMERGENCY POWER

The standby power system shall be a Type 60, Class 2, Level 1 system protected by fire barriers having a minimum 2-hour fire resistance rating. Wiring shall be in accordance with DCD requirements the following standby power loads:

- Fire service elevators
- Emergency mechanical air handling systems (i.e., smoke proof enclosures)
- Electric fire pumps (where both main & standby pumps are electric)
- Emergency command centre equipment and lighting

SOLAR POWER GENERATION SYSTEMS  
In accordance to chapter 14, Section 2.2.3. of UAE Fire code 2018. The Fire and Life Safety requirements for Solar Power Generation Systems shall be as per Table 14.2. following shall be complied 
with.  
 
1. FLAME SPREAD CHARACTERS- 
 
1. FLAME SPREAD CHARACTERISTICS OF PV CELLS ON ROOF 
i. 
The minimum requirement for Solar PV cells to be installed on roof of Lowrise buildings and Private Villas is Class B or equivalent class when tested to test standards as required by Section 
2.2.4. of chapter 14, UAE Fire code 2018. 
ii. For Midrise, Highrise and Super Highrise buildings, the minimum requirement for solar PV cells to be installed on roof is Class A, when tested to test standards as per Section 2.2.4.  of chapter 
14, UAE Fire code 2018. 
iii. Where solar panels are installed on combustible roofs, such combustible roofs shall be separated from the solar panels, cabling and installations by noncombustible material in accordance with 
Chapter 1. Section 7.1.44. 
 
2. FLAME SPREAD CHARACTERISTICS OF PV CELLS ON FACADE 
i. 
The minimum requirement for Solar PV cells to be installed on building façade of any building of any occupancy shall be Class A, when tested to test criteria as per Section 2.2.4. of chapter 14, 
UAE Fire code 2018. 
ii. When solar panels, PV cells are installed on the façade systems, they shall not reduce the structural integrity or the fire resistance rating of the exterior walls. iii. The solar panels and PV cells 
installed on building envelope, shall demonstrate through large scale system tests that the building envelope is not altered in its fire resistance rating. See Section 2.2.4.  of chapter 14, UAE 
Fire code 2018 for fire test requirements. 
 
2. CONSTRUCTION- 
 
1. ACCESS 
i. 
Solar panel installations on roof or on commercial scale as generation plant shall provide access to fire fighters around the installations. 
ii. 
Minimum of 1.2 m wide access path shall be available around the installations. 
iii. 
Solar installations on roof shall be such that access and emergency operations from first responders and fire fighters operations and maneuvering is not jeopardized. 
iv. 
Commercial installations on ground such as Solar power plants shall have 6 m clear fire access around the installations and in between arrays in full compliance with Chapter 2. Fire Access, 
taking into account the dead ends, maximum distance, turning radius etc. 
 
2. SECURITY 
i. 
Commercial Solar Panel installations shall be secured with fence and CCTV monitoring to prevent accidental entry of people and animals. Accidental trespassing of people and animals into such 
installations could result in loss of life and damage to installations. 
ii. 
In domestic installations such as on roofs, facility management should take precautions to isolate and restrict access to such areas to prevent accidental trespassing of children and people, 
endangering their lives. 
 
3. LOCATION 
 
i. 
The solar panel installations on roof shall not obstruct the Smoke control system (Pressurization system, smoke extract system etc.) equipment and components such as air intake openings and 
fans. Obstructions to smoke control system components can severely affect the efficiency and intent of the smoke control strate-gy of the buildings. 
ii. solar panel installations shall not obstruct any building means of egress.  
iii. Solar panel installations shall not obstruct access to LPG Tanks, if any. 
iv. Solar panel installations shall not obstruct building HVAC components.

SOLAR POWER GENERATION SYSTEMS

In accordance to chapter 14, Section 2.2.3. of UAE Fire code 2018. To comply with.

1. FLAME SPREAD CHARACTERISTICS-
1. FLAME SPREAD CHARACTERISTICS OF PV CELLS ON ROOF
i. The minimum requirement for Solar PV cells to be installed on roof shall be in accordance with 2.2.4. of chapter 14, UAE Fire code 2018.

2. FLAME SPREAD CHARACTERISTICS OF PV CELLS ON FACADE
i. The minimum requirement for Solar PV cells to be installed on building envelope shall be in accordance with 2.2.4. of chapter 14, UAE Fire code 2018.
ii. When solar panels, PV cells are installed on the façade systems, the fire resistance of the building envelope, shall demonstrate through large scale fire test in accordance with 2.2.4. of chapter 14, UAE Fire code 2018 for fire test requirements.

4. MEANS OF EGRESS 
 
i. Other than Private Villas and Commercial Villas, solar installations shall have access to two permanent building exit stairs, designed as per Chapter 3. Means of Egress. 
ii. Solar installations having access with temporary ladders, spiral stairs and open steel stairs are not acceptable. 
 
3. SUBSTATIONS- 
 
i. Substations, if any, shall comply with Section 2.1. of chapter 14, UAE Fire code 2018. 
 
4. EQUIPMENT- 
 
i. 
Transformers, if any, shall comply with Section 2.1. of chapter 14, UAE Fire code 2018. 
ii. Inverters shall be listed and approved as per Section 2.2.3.7. 
 
5. SYSTEM IDENTIFICATION- 
 
i. 
Equipment and the system shall have clear identification label near the control system or Command Center or reception, stating if the type of solar system is “Thermal” or “Photovoltaic” and 
the associated warning and safety precautions to be taken during fire accidents. 
ii. 
Solar components release hazardous and toxic elements when they decompose in fire. These warning details shall be identified and made visible with warning signs. 
iii. 
Color coding and standard identification of energized parts of solar system shall be in place to identify “ON” parts such as electrical wire conduits, panel segments etc. 
 
6. EMERGENCY SHUTDOWN- 
 
i. 
In large solar power generation installations, adequate module and array level isolation (disconnect facilities) shall be provided segment wise strategically. Remote shutdown features of modules 
and arrays shall be considered to enable emergency responders and fire fighters, to safely isolate energized segments and successfully execute fire fighting operations. 
ii. In domestic applications, module and array disconnect switches shall be provided on roof. 
iii. Disconnect switches for solar system shall be clearly labeled and distinct from any other devices such as “Manual Call Points”, “Emergency LPG Shut-off” and activation buttons of “Fire suppression 
systems”. 
iv. Location of disconnect switches, inverters and arrangement of solar arrays shall be depicted in a “Site layout” plan and shall be posted at key locations such as control rooms, emergency 
command centers, building receptions etc. 
 
7. FIRE DETECTION AND ALARM SYSTEM- 
 
i. 
Fire Detection and Alarm shall be provided to all the ancillary buildings such as control room, equipment room, storage room and inverter rooms of the solar energy generation plants and 
commercial installations, in accordance with Chapter 8: Fire Detection and Alarm System. 
ii. Main FACP shall be located in control room with annunciator panel at security guard room or main panel shall be located at security guard room. 
 
8. FIRE EXTINGUISHERS- 
 
i. 
In solar energy generating plants, Fire extinguishers shall be provided throughout ancillary buildings in accordance with Chapter 4: Fire Extinguishers. 
ii. 
Trolley type, wheeled CO2 extinguishers, in accordance with Chapter 4, Table 4.3.5., shall be provided at strategic locations throughout large outdoor solar panel installations in an enclosed 
protected outdoor cabinet.
